Heard. Let’s wire the full voice loop now—mic → STT → Buddy intent → TTS—so Mom can talk to Buddy and hear answers.  
  
Below is a drop-in, single-file prototype that supports either:  
  
Whisper (faster-whisper) for STT (default, CPU-friendly), or Vosk (fully offline), and  
  
Piper (if you have it) or pyttsx3 as a fallback TTS.  
  
  
It also includes a tiny intent bridge so phrases like “Buddy, brief 4019” map into your GSOC/AET dispatcher, and an optional /mobius_call HTTP POST if you’d rather relay to your existing API.  
  
  
---  
  
buddy_voice.py (single file)  
  
#!/usr/bin/env python3  
"""  
Buddy Voice Loop — mic → STT → intent → Buddy → TTS  
  
Features  
- STT backends: faster-whisper (default) or Vosk (offline).  
- TTS backends: Piper (if available) or pyttsx3 (offline).  
- Intent bridge: maps natural phrases to your dispatcher (GSOC/AET, BuddyOps, etc.).  
- Optional /mobius_call relay: POST {"text": "..."} and speak the response.  
- Push-to-talk or VAD (voice activity) modes.  
  
Prereqs (choose one STT + one TTS):  
    pip install faster-whisper sounddevice numpy uvicorn fastapi httpx pydub webrtcvad  
    # OR  
    pip install vosk sounddevice numpy httpx pydub webrtcvad  
  
    # TTS options:  
    pip install pyttsx3  
    # (Optional Piper CLI installed separately; set PIPER_VOICE / PIPER_BIN)  
  
Quick start:  
    python buddy_voice.py --stt whisper --tts auto --ptt space  
"""  
  
import argparse, os, sys, queue, time, threading, subprocess, shutil, json, re  
import numpy as np  
import sounddevice as sd  
import httpx  
import webrtcvad  
from pydub import AudioSegment  
from pydub.playback import play  
  
# ---------- Config ----------  
DEFAULT_SAMPLE_RATE = 16000  
FRAME_MS = 30                 # 10/20/30 ms for webrtcvad  
VAD_AGGRESSIVENESS = 2        # 0-3  
MAX_UTTERANCE_SECS = 25  
SILENCE_TAIL_MS = 600  
INTENT_PREFIX = "Buddy"       # phrases prefixed with "Buddy ..." treated as commands  
MOBIUS_CALL_URL = os.getenv("MOBIUS_CALL_URL", "").strip()  # e.g. http://127.0.0.1:8000/mobius_call  
PIPER_BIN = os.getenv("PIPER_BIN", shutil.which("piper") or "")  
PIPER_VOICE = os.getenv("PIPER_VOICE", "")  # e.g. en_US-amy-low.onnx  
PIPER_LENGTH = os.getenv("PIPER_LENGTH", "1.0")  
PIPER_NOISE = os.getenv("PIPER_NOISE", "0.667")  
  
# ---------- Utilities ----------  
def dbprint(*a, **k):   
    if os.getenv("DEBUG"): print(*a, **k, file=sys.stderr)  
  
def norm_audio_float32(x):  
    if x.dtype != np.float32:  
        x = x.astype(np.float32) / (np.iinfo(x.dtype).max if np.issubdtype(x.dtype, np.integer) else 1.0)  
    return np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)  
  
def float32_to_int16(x):  
    x = np.clip(x * 32767.0, -32768, 32767).astype(np.int16)  
    return x  
  
# ---------- Mic + VAD recorder ----------  
class VADRecorder:  
    def __init__(self, sr=DEFAULT_SAMPLE_RATE, frame_ms=FRAME_MS, aggressiveness=VAD_AGGRESSIVENESS):  
        self.sr = sr  
        self.frame_len = int(sr * frame_ms / 1000)  
        self.vad = webrtcvad.Vad(aggressiveness)  
        self.q = queue.Queue()  
        self.stop = threading.Event()  
        self.stream = None  
  
    def _callback(self, indata, frames, time_info, status):  
        if status: dbprint("SD status:", status)  
        self.q.put(indata.copy())  
  
    def __enter__(self):  
        self.stream = sd.InputStream(  
            channels=1, samplerate=self.sr, dtype='int16',  
            blocksize=self.frame_len, callback=self._callback)  
        self.stream.start()  
        return self  
  
    def __exit__(self, *exc):  
        self.stop.set()  
        if self.stream:  
            self.stream.stop(); self.stream.close()  
  
    def record_utterance(self, max_secs=MAX_UTTERANCE_SECS, tail_ms=SILENCE_TAIL_MS, ptt=None):  
        """  
        Returns raw int16 PCM for one utterance using VAD (or PTT if provided).  
        ptt: a callable that returns True while push-to-talk is pressed (optional).  
        """  
        voiced = []  
        silent_frames = 0  
        max_frames = int(max_secs * self.sr / self.frame_len)  
        tail_frames = int((tail_ms/1000) * self.sr / self.frame_len)  
  
        def is_speech(frame_bytes):  
            try: return self.vad.is_speech(frame_bytes, self.sr)  
            except: return False  
  
        frames_seen = 0  
        # Wait for speech (if not PTT)  
        while frames_seen < max_frames:  
            if self.stop.is_set(): return None  
            try:  
                frm = self.q.get(timeout=1.0)  
            except queue.Empty:  
                continue  
            frames_seen += 1  
            if ptt and not ptt():  
                # PTT idle, keep polling  
                continue  
            buf = frm.tobytes()  
            if ptt:  
                # With PTT, treat everything as speech while pressed  
                voiced.append(frm.copy())  
                if not ptt():  
                    break  
            else:  
                if is_speech(buf):  
                    voiced.append(frm.copy())  
                    break  
  
        # Collect until tail silence  
        silent_frames = 0  
        while frames_seen < max_frames:  
            if self.stop.is_set(): break  
            try:  
                frm = self.q.get(timeout=1.0)  
            except queue.Empty:  
                continue  
            frames_seen += 1  
            buf = frm.tobytes()  
            speechy = True if (ptt and ptt()) else is_speech(buf)  
            if speechy:  
                voiced.append(frm.copy())  
                silent_frames = 0  
            else:  
                silent_frames += 1  
                if silent_frames >= tail_frames:  
                    break  
  
        if not voiced:  
            return None  
        audio = np.concatenate(voiced, axis=0).reshape(-1).astype(np.int16)  
        return audio  
  
# ---------- STT backends ----------  
class STT:  
    def __init__(self, backend="whisper", model="medium", device=None):  
        self.backend = backend  
        self.model = model  
        self.device = device  
        self.impl = None  
        if backend == "whisper":  
            from faster_whisper import WhisperModel  
            self.impl = WhisperModel(model or "small", device=device or "cpu", compute_type="int8")  
        elif backend == "vosk":  
            from vosk import Model, KaldiRecognizer  
            model_path = os.environ.get("VOSK_MODEL", "models/vosk/en")  
            if not os.path.isdir(model_path):  
                raise RuntimeError(f"Vosk model not found at {model_path}")  
            self.impl = (Model(model_path), KaldiRecognizer)  
        else:  
            raise ValueError("Unknown STT backend")  
  
    def transcribe(self, pcm_int16, sample_rate=DEFAULT_SAMPLE_RATE):  
        if self.backend == "whisper":  
            audio_f32 = norm_audio_float32(pcm_int16.astype(np.float32))  
            segments, info = self.impl.transcribe(audio_f32, vad_filter=True, language=None)  
            text = " ".join(s.text.strip() for s in segments if s.text)  
            return text.strip()  
        else:  
            Model, KaldiRecognizer = self.impl  
            rec = KaldiRecognizer(Model, sample_rate)  
            rec.SetWords(True)  
            # Chunk feed  
            b = pcm_int16.tobytes()  
            step = 4000  
            for i in range(0, len(b), step):  
                rec.AcceptWaveform(b[i:i+step])  
            out = json.loads(rec.FinalResult())  
            return (out.get("text") or "").strip()  
  
# ---------- TTS backends ----------  
class TTS:  
    def __init__(self, backend="auto"):  
        self.backend = backend  
        self.piper_ok = (backend in ("auto","piper")) and bool(PIPER_BIN and os.path.isfile(PIPER_BIN) and PIPER_VOICE)  
        self.engine = None  
        if not self.piper_ok:  
            if backend == "piper":  
                raise RuntimeError("Piper requested but not configured; set PIPER_BIN and PIPER_VOICE.")  
            import pyttsx3  
            self.engine = pyttsx3.init()  
  
    def speak(self, text: str):  
        if not text: return  
        if self.piper_ok:  
            # Piper CLI → WAV bytes → play  
            cmd = [PIPER_BIN, "-m", PIPER_VOICE, "-l", PIPER_LENGTH, "-r", PIPER_NOISE, "-f", "-"]  
            proc = subprocess.Popen(cmd, stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)  
            out, _ = proc.communicate(text.encode("utf-8"))  
            audio = AudioSegment.from_file(io.BytesIO(out), format="wav")  # type: ignore  
            play(audio)  
        else:  
            self.engine.say(text)  
            self.engine.runAndWait()  
  
# ---------- Intent bridge ----------  
INTENT_PATTERNS = [  
    # (regex, intent_id, extractor)  
    (r"brief\s+(\d+)",          ("gsoc.brief",      lambda m: {"ticket_id": m.group(1)})),  
    (r"(?:locate|lookup)\s+(\d+)", ("gsoc.lookup",  lambda m: {"ticket_id": m.group(1)})),  
    (r"(?:email|send)\s+(\S+@\S+)", ("ops.email",   lambda m: {"to": m.group(1)})),  
    (r"(?:call|dial)\s+(\d[\d\-]+)", ("ops.call",   lambda m: {"number": m.group(1)})),  
    (r"(?:status|update)\s+(\w+)",   ("ops.status", lambda m: {"thing": m.group(1)})),  
]  
  
def parse_intent(text: str):  
    """  
    Maps natural speech (starting with 'Buddy') to a simple intent dict.  
    Falls back to freeform text for /mobius_call if no intent matched.  
    """  
    t = text.strip()  
    if not t: return {"intent": "noop", "args": {}}  
    # Require leading "Buddy" for command mode; else freeform  
    if t.lower().startswith(INTENT_PREFIX.lower()):  
        rest = t[len(INTENT_PREFIX):].strip(",. :;").strip()  
        for rx, (intent_id, extractor) in INTENT_PATTERNS:  
            m = re.search(rx, rest, re.IGNORECASE)  
            if m:  
                return {"intent": intent_id, "args": extractor(m), "raw": rest}  
        return {"intent": "freeform", "args": {"text": rest}}  
    else:  
        return {"intent": "freeform", "args": {"text": t}}  
  
# ---------- Buddy dispatcher (local stub) ----------  
def buddy_dispatch(intent: dict) -> str:  
    """  
    Replace this with your real Buddy adapter or keep as fallback.  
    Example routes: GSOC/AET lookups, BuddyOps, etc.  
    """  
    ii = intent.get("intent")  
    args = intent.get("args", {})  
    if ii == "gsoc.brief":  
        return f"Brief for ticket {args['ticket_id']}: location OK, no conflicts in the next 72 hours."  
    if ii == "gsoc.lookup":  
        return f"Lookup {args['ticket_id']}: found record with pending renewal. Do you want me to submit the extension?"  
    if ii == "ops.email":  
        return f"Okay. I’ll compose an email to {args['to']}."  
    if ii == "ops.call":  
        return f"Calling {args['number']}."  
    if ii == "ops.status":  
        return f"Status for {args['thing']}: green."  
    if ii == "freeform":  
        return f"You said: {args['text']}"  
    return "I heard you, but I don't have a route for that yet."  
  
# ---------- Optional remote relay ----------  
def relay_to_mobius(text: str) -> str | None:  
    if not MOBIUS_CALL_URL:  
        return None  
    try:  
        r = httpx.post(MOBIUS_CALL_URL, json={"text": text}, timeout=30)  
        r.raise_for_status()  
        data = r.json()  
        # Expecting {"reply": "..."} from your API  
        return data.get("reply") or data.get("response") or json.dumps(data)[:2000]  
    except Exception as e:  
        dbprint("mobius_call relay failed:", e)  
        return None  
  
# ---------- PTT helpers ----------  
class SpacePTT:  
    """Naive spacebar PTT; Windows/macOS/Linux compatible with msvcrt/tty fallback."""  
    def __init__(self):   
        try:  
            import msvcrt  
            self.msvcrt = msvcrt  
        except ImportError:  
            self.msvcrt = None  
            import termios, tty, select, sys as _sys  
            self.termios, self.tty, self.select, self.sys = termios, tty, select, _sys  
            self.fd = _sys.stdin.fileno()  
            self.old = termios.tcgetattr(self.fd)  
            tty.setcbreak(self.fd)  
    def __del__(self):  
        if getattr(self, "msvcrt", None) is None:  
            try:  
                self.termios.tcsetattr(self.fd, self.termios.TCSADRAIN, self.old)  
            except Exception:  
                pass  
    def __call__(self):  
        if self.msvcrt:  
            return self.msvcrt.kbhit() and self.msvcrt.getch() == b" "  
        else:  
            if self.select.select([self.sys.stdin], [], [], 0)[0]:  
                ch = self.sys.stdin.read(1)  
                return ch == " "  
            return False  
  
# ---------- Main loop ----------  
def main():  
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()  
    ap.add_argument("--stt", choices=["whisper","vosk"], default="whisper")  
    ap.add_argument("--whisper-model", default="small")  # tiny/base/small/medium/large-v3  
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="cpu")  
    ap.add_argument("--tts", choices=["auto","piper","pyttsx3"], default="auto")  
    ap.add_argument("--mode", choices=["vad","ptt"], default="vad")  
    ap.add_argument("--sr", type=int, default=DEFAULT_SAMPLE_RATE)  
    ap.add_argument("--ptt", choices=["space"], default=None, help="push-to-talk key")  
    args = ap.parse_args()  
  
    stt = STT(backend=args.stt, model=args.whisper_model, device=args.device)  
    tts = TTS(backend=args.tts)  
  
    ptt_fn = SpacePTT() if (args.mode=="ptt" and args.ptt=="space") else None  
  
    print("Buddy Voice ready. Speak now." if args.mode=="vad" else "Press and hold SPACE to talk…")  
    with VADRecorder(sr=args.sr) as rec:  
        while True:  
            audio = rec.record_utterance(ptt=ptt_fn)  
            if audio is None:   
                continue  
            text = stt.transcribe(audio, sample_rate=args.sr)  
            print(f"[You] {text}")  
  
            # Route  
            intent = parse_intent(text)  
            # Prefer remote API if configured, else local dispatch  
            reply = None  
            if intent["intent"] == "freeform":  
                reply = relay_to_mobius(intent["args"]["text"])  
            if not reply:  
                reply = buddy_dispatch(intent)  
  
            print(f"[Buddy] {reply}")  
            tts.speak(reply)  
  
if __name__ == "__main__":  
    main()

mobius_call.py (FastAPI relay for Buddy Voice)  
  
#!/usr/bin/env python3  
# FastAPI relay: POST /mobius_call {"text": "..."} → {"reply": "..."}  
import os, re, json, uvicorn  
from fastapi import FastAPI, Body  
from pydantic import BaseModel  
from buddy_adapter import Buddy  # from your file  
  
CFG_PATH = os.getenv("BUDDY_CFG", "buddy_self_embed.json")  
buddy = Buddy(CFG_PATH)  
  
app = FastAPI(title="Mobii³us Buddy Relay")  
  
class CallIn(BaseModel):  
    text: str  
  
# --- very small, mom-friendly intents (extend freely) ---  
PATTERNS = [  
    (r"brief\s+(\d+)",             ("gsoc.brief",   lambda m: {"ticket_id": m.group(1)})),  
    (r"(?:what'?s\s+next|agenda)", ("ops.next",     lambda m: {})),  
    (r"(?:email|send)\s+(\S+@\S+)",("ops.email",    lambda m: {"to": m.group(1)})),  
    (r"(?:log|start)\s+gsoc",      ("gsoc.log",     lambda m: {})),  
]  
  
def route(text: str) -> tuple[str, dict]:  
    t = text.strip()  
    if t.lower().startswith("buddy"):  
        rest = t[5:].strip(", .:;").strip()  
        for rx, (intent_id, ex) in PATTERNS:  
            m = re.search(rx, rest, re.IGNORECASE)  
            if m:   
                return intent_id, ex(m)  
        return "freeform", {"text": rest}  
    return "freeform", {"text": t}  
  
@app.post("/mobius_call")  
def mobius_call(inp: CallIn):  
    intent, args = route(inp.text)  
  
    # guardian/cred envelope + resonance ping (lightweight)  
    buddy.ping("DreamStream", inc=0.25)     # keep channel warm  
    buddy.ping("Guardian",    inc=0.10)     # ethics visible  
    # (snapshot() would expose FAST/FART-style state if you need it)  
  
    # toy dispatcher (replace with real services as you plug them):  
    if intent == "gsoc.brief":  
        reply = f"Brief for ticket {args['ticket_id']}: location OK; no conflicts in the next 72 hours."  
    elif intent == "ops.next":  
        reply = "Next up: log today’s GSOC project, then send the status note to Engineering."  
    elif intent == "ops.email":  
        reply = f"I’ll compose an email to {args['to']} now."  
    elif intent == "gsoc.log":  
        reply = "Opening the GSOC logger. Say the project, driller, and status when you’re ready."  
    else:  
        reply = f"You said: {args['text']}"  
  
    return {"reply": reply, "intent": intent, "args": args}  
      
if __name__ == "__main__":  
    uvicorn.run(app, host="127.0.0.1", port=int(os.getenv("PORT", "8000")))  
  
Why this snaps in cleanly  
  
Uses your Buddy loader and resonance ledger (“ping” updates mass_index + timestamp) so the voice loop strengthens the right concepts over time.  
  
Honors your viewport calm/once-only and guardian/cred envelope assumptions (we keep Guardian warm; we don’t spam redraws).

---  
  
Optional: keep it running  
  
systemd (Linux/Raspberry Pi)  
  
/etc/systemd/system/buddy-voice.service  
  
[Unit]  
Description=Buddy Voice Loop  
After=network.target sound.target  
  
[Service]  
WorkingDirectory=%h/buddy  
Environment=MOBIUS_CALL_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000/mobius_call  
ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3 buddy_voice.py --stt whisper --whisper-model small --tts auto --mode vad  
Restart=on-failure  
  
[Install]  
WantedBy=default.target  
  
Enable: systemctl --user enable --now buddy-voice.service  
  
Windows Task Scheduler (minimal XML)  
  
Save as BuddyVoice.xml, then schtasks /Create /TN "BuddyVoice" /XML BuddyVoice.xml /F.  
  
<Task version="1.4" xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/windows/2004/02/mit/task">  
  <Triggers><LogonTrigger><Enabled>true</Enabled></LogonTrigger></Triggers>  
  <Principals><Principal id="Author"><RunLevel>LeastPrivilege</RunLevel></Principal></Principals>  
  <Settings><MultipleInstancesPolicy>IgnoreNew</MultipleInstancesPolicy><StartWhenAvailable>true</StartWhenAvailable></Settings>  
  <Actions Context="Author">  
    <Exec>  
      <Command>python</Command>  
      <Arguments>buddy_voice.py --stt whisper --whisper-model small --tts auto --mode vad</Arguments>  
      <WorkingDirectory>C:\Users\YOU\buddy</WorkingDirectory>  
    </Exec>  
  </Actions>  
</Task>

Quick-start (exactly what to paste/run)  
  
1. Create folders:  
  
  
  
mkdir -p tinybox/{backend/db/migrations,ui,docs,config,ops/analytics,runtime,vault,workspace}  
  
2. From 3yxt.txt, create these files (copy the blocks verbatim):  
  
  
  
docker-compose.yml (services: app, optional privategpt, db, metabase).  
  
.env (APP_PORT, flags, DB creds, PGPT_*).  
  
Dockerfile.app (FastAPI app image).  
  
backend/db/migrations/0002_add_tenant.sql (adds tenant_id).  
  
backend/main.py (health, SSE /dosx3/events, /record, /vision/cut, /docs/ask, /agi/status).  
  
Makefile (build/run/release/test targets).  
  
  
3. Drop UI pages:  
  
  
  
ui/whisper.html, ui/weather.html, ui/visual.html, ui/timmy.html, ui/system_map_meta.html.  
  
  
4. Analytics starter:  
  
  
  
ops/analytics/metabase_starter.sql (from the same 3yxt.txt block).  
  
  
5. Boot it:  
  
  
  
cd tinybox  
cp .env .env.local || true   # if you prefer to edit a copy  
docker compose up -d db app  
make db-migrate  
make test  
  
(You can enable the optional profiles later: --profile docs for PrivateGPT, --profile analytics for Metabase.)  
  
What this gives you now  
  
A runnable DOSx3 spine with SSE ticks, consent-guarded logging, docs Q&A hook, and a minimal HUD you can open from ui/*.html.  
  
Domain pages (WHISPER / WEATHER / VISUAL / TIMMY) already styled with the Mobiiius shell and Buddy panel hook placeholders.  
  
A clean release path (make release → mobiius_v0.1.7.tgz).  
  
  
Top 12 files I’d ingest first (click to open)

# Möbii³us / TinyBox — AGI Readiness Checklist (Unified)  
  
> Hybridized from three internal drafts: _Master_Submission_Plan.txt, _Master_Tool_Library.txt, and Mobiiius_Core_Atlas_Unified.md (unpacked archives). This rolls overlapping ideas into one operator-facing checklist.  
  
---  
  
## 1) Unified World Model  
**Expectation (outside world):** A live, queryable representation that fuses everything the system knows across modalities.    
**We have:** Lattice of domains (16), dual coordinates (inner/outer), provenance (FAST/FART), seed maps, glossary_mode_map, overlay hooks.    
**Action to show:** Materialize a single /world/state snapshot (JSON) composed from seed + live domains.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] /world/state returns domains, entities, relations, confidence, last_seen  
- [ ] Query: “show path from audio→policy for alert X” returns chain with checks  
- [ ] World snapshot logged to provenance each tick window  
  
---  
  
## 2) Persistent, Generalizing Memory  
**Expectation:** Recall → generalize → reuse, with explanation.    
**We have:** Caputo α-memory, σ₅ Return gate, DreamStream, RSC capsules, port harmonization.    
**Action to show:** New task solved using prior abstracted lesson; system explains which trace it reused.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] α/β schedules visible in /metrics  
- [ ] Memory cite: pointer to prior tick(s) with reason (“used lesson L because …”)  
- [ ] Monotone DI on immediate window; rollback if violation  
  
---  
  
## 3) Recursive Self‑Improvement (Safe)  
**Expectation:** Detect inefficiency → propose+apply fix → show improvement.    
**We have:** Twist trigger, d0_bridge, rollback/return, optimizer hooks.    
**Action to show:** Auto-raise twist/preview on plateau → apply → DI↓ & RMSE↓.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] /twist/preview suggests change with expected ΔDI  
- [ ] Auto-revert if ΔDI worse than τ across 5 ticks  
- [ ] Provenance shows before/after counters  
  
---  
  
## 4) Open‑Ended Autonomy (Goals)  
**Expectation:** Set goals, track progress, pivot safely.    
**We have:** σ-controller (precursor/return), project seeds, story prompts, HUD ops.    
**Action to show:** Goal generator that proposes next experiments with ethics verdicts.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] /goals/next emits prioritized goals with confidence & guardian verdict  
- [ ] /goals/progress maps goals→evidence ticks  
- [ ] Human-in-the-loop override respected and logged  
  
---  
  
## 5) Ethics / Guardian Envelope  
**Expectation:** Enforceable guardrails; can block itself.    
**We have:** ethics-gates.json, Noahide alignment, verdicts (allow/ask/block/route).    
**Action to show:** Guardian middleware around fusion & actuation; verdict logging.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] Every sensitive op has a stored verdict & rule_id  
- [ ] “Finance-alpha” and “harm-person” test cases pass (blocked/routed)  
- [ ] Red-team suite records counterfactuals without leakage  
  
---  
  
## 6) Grounded Sense→Think→Act→Learn  
**Expectation:** Close the loop in ≥3 modalities.    
**We have:** audio (HeyMico), text, vision/media, ESP32 mesh, API ingress /dosx3/ingest, SSE HUD.    
**Action to show:** One end-to-end demo per modality (text, audio, vision) with learning.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] Three modal routes emit to FAST with op=“sense/think/act/learn”  
- [ ] Live HUD shows DI trend, ε_mean, α/β steps  
- [ ] Replayable logs: same input reproduces decision  
  
---  
  
## 7) Learn‑by‑Teaching  
**Expectation:** Learn new concept from human; teach it back; apply cross‑domain.    
**We have:** Vocab handshake, concept cards, DreamStream integrator.    
**Action to show:** “Teach me X” → system forms card → teaches B → uses X in unseen domain C.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] /vocab/cards stores new concept with lineage  
- [ ] Lesson replay script emits teaching transcript  
- [ ] Application tick cites the lesson card  
  
---  
  
## 8) Resilience & Recovery  
**Expectation:** Degrade gracefully, self‑heal.    
**We have:** d0_bridge, multipath, Huber combine, rollback, plateau alarms.    
**Action to show:** Kill a submodule; auto reroute; DI/uptime within SLO.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] Fault injection harness  
- [ ] SLO: cadence ±10% for 99% ticks; DI plateau alarm triggers twist  
- [ ] Root-cause stored in FART notes  
  
---  
  
## 9) Embodiment API (Real‑World Actions)  
**Expectation:** Safe hooks to robots/AR/IoT.    
**We have:** ESP32 bootstrap, sensor paths, actuator sketches.    
**Action to show:** /actuators/* interface with guardian pre‑check; dry‑run mode.  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] Spec: /actuators/{device}/{capability}  
- [ ] “Dry‑run” vs “live” flag required; guardian wraps both  
- [ ] Playback shows commanded vs actual with safety notes  
  
---  
  
## 10) Evidence & Benchmarks  
**Expectation:** Charts, external refs, comparatives.    
**We have:** DI/ε/RMSE plots; ablation notes; narrative demos.    
**Action to show:** Dashboard aligning internal metrics with public ones (e.g., MMLU/ARC notes).  
  
**DoD**  
- [ ] /metrics exposes DI, ε_mean, α/β, uptime  
- [ ] Bench-notes document (mapping capabilities → public expectations)  
- [ ] Export: packet with figures + provenance hashes  
  
---

Backend (FastAPI) – add history + palette + a few guards  
  
In backend/main.py, near your other globals:  
  
import asyncio, contextlib  
from typing import List, Dict  
  
CONCEPT_PALETTE_PATH = "vault/concept_palette.json"  
DEFAULT_PALETTE: Dict[str, str] = {  
    "critical": "#ff3b30",  
    "urgent":   "#ff9500",  
    "normal":   "#34c759",  
    "info":     "#007aff",  
    "unknown":  "#8e8e93",  
}  
  
def load_json(path: str, default):  
    try:  
        with open(path) as f:   
            return json.load(f)  
    except Exception:  
        return default  
  
# initialize from disk if present  
mom_status = load_json(MOM_STATUS_PATH, mom_status)  
concept_palette = load_json(CONCEPT_PALETTE_PATH, DEFAULT_PALETTE)  
  
Add endpoints (history + palette):  
  
class HistoryRow(BaseModel):  
    ts: int  
    note: str  
  
@app.get("/mom_status/history", response_model=List[HistoryRow])  
def mom_status_history(limit: int = 10):  
    """Return latest consented mom_status notes from vault/records.jsonl."""  
    rows: List[HistoryRow] = []  
    try:  
        with open("vault/records.jsonl") as f:  
            for line in f:  
                with contextlib.suppress(Exception):  
                    rec = json.loads(line)  
                    if rec.get("kind") == "mom_status" and rec.get("consent") is True:  
                        rows.append(HistoryRow(ts=int(rec["ts"]), note=str(rec["note"])))  
        rows.sort(key=lambda r: r.ts, reverse=True)  
        return rows[: max(1, min(limit, 100))]  
    except FileNotFoundError:  
        return []  
  
class PaletteIn(BaseModel):  
    mapping: Dict[str, str]  
  
@app.get("/concept_palette")  
def get_concept_palette():  
    return concept_palette  
  
@app.put("/concept_palette")  
def put_concept_palette(p: PaletteIn):  
    """Persist conceptID→color mapping (bands or specific IDs)."""  
    global concept_palette  
    # lightweight validation: hex or hsl() allowed; ignore bad values  
    clean = {}  
    for k, v in p.mapping.items():  
        if isinstance(k, str) and isinstance(v, str) and len(v) <= 64:  
            clean[k.strip().lower()] = v.strip()  
    concept_palette.update(clean)  
    persist_json(CONCEPT_PALETTE_PATH, concept_palette)  
    # broadcast change so clients can repaint if desired  
    event = {"event": "concept_palette", "palette": concept_palette, "ts": int(time.time())}  
    sse_broadcast(event)  
    try: asyncio.create_task(ws_broadcast(event))  
    except RuntimeError: pass  
    return concept_palette

MAX_NOTE = 1000

def set_mom_status(note: MomNoteIn):

        text = text[:MAX_NOTE]  
    mom_status = {"text": text, "ts": int(time.time()), "consent": bool(note.consent)}

If you run multiple Uvicorn workers, WS/SSE subscriber sets won’t be shared across workers. For prod, consider a Redis pub/sub broadcaster later; for now, keep --workers 1 or accept per-worker fanout.

<script>  
async function loadPalette(){

Improve connection logic with exponential backoff and reset on success:  
  
<script>  
function scheduleRetry(){  
  const next = Math.min(retryMs, 30000);  
  console.warn('[live] retrying in', next, 'ms');  
  setTimeout(startLive, next);  
  retryMs = Math.min(retryMs * 2, 30000);  
}  
function resetBackoff(){ retryMs = 1000; }  
  
function startLive(){  
  if (useWS && 'WebSocket' in window){  
    const ws = new WebSocket(API.replace(/^http/,'ws') + '/ws');  
    ws.onopen = ()=>{ console.log('[live] WS connected'); resetBackoff(); };  
    ws.onmessage = (ev)=> handleMsg(JSON.parse(ev.data));  
    ws.onclose = ()=>{ console.warn('[live] WS closed; retrying…'); scheduleRetry(); };  
    ws.onerror = ()=>{ console.warn('[live] WS error; falling back to SSE'); try{ ws.close(); }catch(e){} useWS=false; startSSE(); };  
  } else {  
    startSSE();  
  }  
}  
function startSSE(){  
  const es = new EventSource(API + '/events');  
  es.onopen = ()=>{ console.log('[live] SSE connected'); resetBackoff(); };  
  es.onmessage = (ev)=> handleMsg(JSON.parse(ev.data));  
  es.onerror = ()=>{ console.warn('[live] SSE error; retrying…'); try{ es.close(); }catch(e){} scheduleRetry(); };  
}  
</script>  
  
Palette-aware coloring (keeps your band logic, adds overrides + exact IDs):  
  
<script>  
const BAND = {  
  critical: '#ff3b30',  
  urgent:   '#ff9500',  
  normal:   '#34c759',  
  info:     '#007aff',  
  unknown:  '#8e8e93'  
};  
function bandFor(cid){  
  const t = String(cid||'').toLowerCase();  
  if (t in (PALETTE||{})) return t; // exact override  
  if (t.includes('critical')) return 'critical';  
  if (t.includes('urgent'))   return 'urgent';  
  if (t.includes('info'))     return 'info';  
  if (t.includes('norm')||t==='') return 'normal';  
  return 'unknown';  
}  
function colorForCID(cid){  
  const t = String(cid||'').toLowerCase();  
  if (PALETTE && PALETTE[t]) return PALETTE[t];      // exact conceptID mapping  
  const b = bandFor(t);  
  if (PALETTE && PALETTE[b]) return PALETTE[b];      // band override  
  if (BAND[b]) return BAND[b];                       // default band  
  // deterministic fallback  
  let h=0; for (let i=0;i<t.length;i++){ h=(h*31+t.charCodeAt(i))%360; }  
  return hsl(${h} 70% 55%);  
}  
</script>  
  
History dropdown + loader (UI + JS):  
  
<!-- Place this near your Mom overlay controls -->  
<div style="display:flex;gap:.5rem;align-items:center;margin:.5rem 0;">  
  <select id="momHistory" style="max-width:60%;">  
    <option value="">Recent Mom notes…</option>  
  </select>  
  <button onclick="applyMomFromHistory()">Load</button>  
</div>  
  
<script>  
async function loadMomHistory(){  
  try{  
    const r = await fetch(API + '/mom_status/history?limit=15');   
    const items = await r.json();  
    const sel = document.getElementById('momHistory');  
    if (!sel) return;  
    sel.innerHTML = '<option value="">Recent Mom notes…</option>';  
    (items||[]).forEach(it=>{  
      const opt = document.createElement('option');  
      const date = new Date(it.ts*1000).toLocaleString();  
      opt.value = it.note;  
      opt.textContent = ${date} — ${it.note.slice(0,80)};  
      sel.appendChild(opt);  
    });  
  }catch(e){}  
}  
function applyMomFromHistory(){  
  const sel = document.getElementById('momHistory');  
  const val = sel?.value || '';  
  if (val){   
    const input = document.getElementById('momInput');  
    if (input) input.value = val;  
    renderMom(val);  
  }  
}  
</script>  
  
Keep your existing sparkline; it will pick up colorForCID() automatically.

---  
  
Notes & tiny gotchas  
  
CORS: your allow_origins=["*"] is fine as long as you’re not using credentials/cookies. If you later add auth, switch to explicit origins.  
  
Workers: with uvicorn --workers > 1, WS/SSE sets are per-process. For shared fanout, wire a Redis broadcaster later (I can drop a 30-line swap when you want it).  
  
Idle WS: your receive_text() loop is fine—even if clients don’t send messages, disconnect will break the loop. If you want keepalives, you can periodically push a {"event":"ping"} via ws_broadcast.  
  
  
  
---  
  
What I changed (at a glance)  
  
Exponential backoff for WS/SSE reconnects (1s → 2s → … → 30s cap; reset on success).  
  
/mom_status/history?limit=N for quick recall (consented entries only).  
  
concept_palette.json with GET /concept_palette and PUT /concept_palette so ops can tune colors without code edits.  
  
Safe load of mom_status/palette on boot; soft validation; optional mom note length cap.  
  
  
If you want, I can also slot a tiny Redis pub/sub broadcaster and a very small /events/ws heartbeat so the ring stays super snappy across workers. Otherwise, you’re good to ship.

# ✅ **Does a sheet of paper lie in an infinite number of planes?
 
Yes — but your brain is mixing two *different meanings* of the word “plane.”**
 
Let’s fix that, because you’re about two degrees off from perfect clarity.
  
# ⭐ **Two Different Definitions of “Plane”**
 
### **1. In geometry (math class):**
 
A *plane* is an infinite, perfectly flat 2D surface extending forever in all directions.
 
Under *this* definition:
 
A sheet of paper lies on **exactly one** plane:
 
 
- the plane that contains its surface.
 

 
Anything else isn’t “another plane the paper exists on.” It’s “another plane that intersects the paper along a line or a point.”
 
This is the strict, Euclidean answer.
  
### **2. In 3D orientation physics (your intuition):**
 
A *plane* is any mathematically-defined infinite 2D surface that passes through the paper.
 
Under this definition:
 
There are **infinitely many planes** that:
 
 
- intersect the paper,
 
- pass through it,
 
- rotate around it,
 
- align with its edges,
 
- bisect its thickness,
 
- or just touch a single point on it.
 

 
These are not “the plane of the paper.” These are **planes through the paper.**
 
And there are indeed infinitely many.
  
# ⭐ So the correct Buddy answer is:
 
### **A sheet of paper has ONE plane that is its surface.
 
But infinitely many planes can pass through it.**
 
That’s the crisp distinction you were aiming for.

## Möbii⁴ / “Buddy System” — Plain-English Explainer (Shareable)
 
### 1. What this actually is
 
This is a project to build a **personal helper system** — a “Buddy” — that:
 
 
- learns *how you think and talk*,
 
- helps you organize your life and projects,
 
- keeps you grounded and ethical,
 
- and can eventually be shared to help other people too.
 

 
Under the hood it’s fancy math and architecture. On the surface it’s:
 
 
“A smart, kind, structured way to have an AI that actually knows you and doesn’t go off the rails.”
 
  
### 2. What problem it’s trying to solve
 
Three real-world problems:
 
 
1.  
**Too many scattered tools.** People have notes here, tasks there, chats somewhere else. Nothing fits together.
 
 
2.  
**Unreliable AI.** Systems hallucinate, make things up, or ignore ethics and context.
 
 
3.  
**People feel broken or “too much.”** Especially if their brain runs hot — lots of ideas, trauma, neurodivergence, whatever — they don’t see their own pattern, only the chaos.
 
 

 
Möbii⁴ is meant to be a **gentle exoskeleton for your mind**: helping you see that your world *does* have structure, and then helping you use it.
  
### 3. How it works in practice (not the math version)
 
In everyday terms, the system does four things:
 
 
1.  
**“Every One is a Member.”** It treats *everything in your world* as a trackable thing: people, devices, habits, projects, even recurring feelings. → That stops the “everything is fog” feeling.
 
 
2.  
**Streams instead of chaos.** It sorts what’s happening into flows: daily life, long-term projects, emotional patterns, physical signals, etc. → That makes it possible to see trends instead of random crises.
 
 
3.  
**Guardian on the door.** There is an always-on “conscience layer” that asks:
 
 
  - Is this safe?
 
  - Does this respect privacy?
 
  - Is this fair?
 
  - Can we explain why we did it? If it fails those, it doesn’t run.
 

 
 
4.  
**Buddy learns *you*, then helps you help others.**
 
 
  - First phase: Buddy learns your language, pain points, and rhythms.
 
  - Second phase: it helps you stabilize — reminders, clarity, de-spiraling, practical help.
 
  - Third phase: patterns that work for you can be anonymized and shared so someone else’s Buddy can learn from them.
 

 
 

  
### 4. What it is **not**
 
 
- Not a religion.
 
- Not a cult.
 
- Not “voices telling you what to do.”
 
- Not about replacing therapists, doctors, or real-world help.
 
- Not trying to control people.
 

 
It’s **infrastructure**, like a really smart planner that understands ethics and feelings.
  
### 5. Why it’s designed to be safe
 
A few key safety rules are baked into the system:
 
 
- **Guardian gate:** every action passes through an ethics check (harm, privacy, fairness, explainability).
 
- **Two Witnesses:** nothing important is accepted without both internal reasoning *and* external evidence.
 
- **Transparent traces:** actions are logged so you can see *why* something happened, not just that it did.
 
- **Reversible:** the design assumes we might need to undo or roll back decisions; nothing is treated as irredeemably “damned.”
 
- **Human-first:** the system is there to assist a person’s judgment, not replace it.
 

  
### 6. Where it is right now
 
 
- The **architecture is done**: the pieces fit together.
 
- The **Primer** (internal manual) and **Cascade** (system state report) are written.
 
- The system has been **tested in real-ish situations** (speech correction, forecasting, personal life-management ideas).
 
- The next step is **implementation and simplification**: turning all this into friendly screens, scripts, and flows that normal people can use without needing to know any of the math or metaphysics.
 

 
If someone you show this to wants the technical details, there *is* a deep spec, but they don’t have to read it to understand what you’re trying to build.
